Step 3: rewriting the middle-region functional.

Recall the spacetime functional
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}
from (
8.25
).
In the tetrad formalism it is convenient to introduce the
S^{2}
–valued 1–forms

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=i_{\hat{L}}F=\alpha_{t}\,dt+\alpha_{r^{\ast}}\,dr^{\ast}+\alpha_{\theta}\,d\theta+\alpha_{\varphi}\,d\varphi,

\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=i_{\hat{N}}F=\underline{\alpha}_{t}\,dt+\underline{\alpha}_{r^{\ast}}\,dr^{\ast}+\underline{\alpha}_{\theta}\,d\theta+\underline{\alpha}_{\varphi}\,d\varphi.

(C.13)

From the definitions one has

\displaystyle|\Phi_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\theta}^{2}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}\alpha_{\varphi}^{2},

\displaystyle|\Phi_{-1}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{\alpha}_{\theta}^{2}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}\underline{\alpha}_{\varphi}^{2}.

Moreover, the transport identities (
C.8
)–(
C.9
) imply that derivatives of
\Phi_{0}
along
\hat{L}
and
\hat{N}

can be expressed in terms of angular derivatives of
\Phi_{\pm 1}
and current terms.
In particular, expanding the right-hand sides and using the standard Hodge decomposition on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
yields

\displaystyle|\hat{L}\Phi_{0}|^{2}+|\hat{N}\Phi_{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl|\not{\nabla}^{a}\alpha_{a}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\varepsilon^{ab}\not{\nabla}_{a}\alpha_{b}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\not{\nabla}^{a}\underline{\alpha}_{a}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\varepsilon^{ab}\not{\nabla}_{a}\underline{\alpha}_{b}\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigl|r^{2}\bigl(D_{0}\phi\,\overline{\phi}-\phi\,\overline{D_{0}\phi}\bigr)\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|r^{2}\bigl(D_{1}\phi\,\overline{\phi}-\phi\,\overline{D_{1}\phi}\bigr)\bigr|^{2}.

Consequently,
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}
can be bounded by an expression of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}\ \lesssim\ \int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Bigl(

\displaystyle\bigl|\not{\nabla}^{a}\alpha_{a}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\varepsilon^{ab}\not{\nabla}_{a}\alpha_{b}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\not{\nabla}^{a}\underline{\alpha}_{a}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\varepsilon^{ab}\not{\nabla}_{a}\underline{\alpha}_{b}\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle+|r^{2}\phi\,D_{0}\phi|^{2}+|r^{2}\phi\,D_{1}\phi|^{2}

\displaystyle+|D_{\hat{\theta}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{\varphi}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{v}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{w}}\phi|^{2}

\displaystyle+P(\phi)\Bigr)\,\bigl|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}\bigr|\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}\,dt.

(C.14)

Thus, it suffices to establish the angular Hodge bounds

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\Bigl(\bigl|\not{\nabla}^{a}\alpha_{a}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\varepsilon^{ab}\not{\nabla}_{a}\alpha_{b}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr)\bigl|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}\bigr|\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigl|\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}\alpha\bigr|^{2}\bigl|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}\bigr|\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2},

(C.15)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\Bigl(\bigl|\not{\nabla}^{a}\underline{\alpha}_{a}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\varepsilon^{ab}\not{\nabla}_{a}\underline{\alpha}_{b}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr)\bigl|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}\bigr|\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigl|\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}\underline{\alpha}\bigr|^{2}\bigl|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}\bigr|\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}.

as well as the scalar bound

\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\bigl(|\phi\,D_{0}\phi|^{2}+|\phi\,D_{1}\phi|^{2}\bigr)\,\bigl|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}\bigr|\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}\ \lesssim\ E_{0}.

(C.16)

Here
E_{0}
is the endpoint energy quantity

E_{0}:=\Bigl(|\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t_{i})|+|\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t_{i+1})|+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigl(|\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t_{i})|+|\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t_{i+1})|\bigr)\Bigr),

(C.17)

where the
reduced energy
is defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{t}}(1-\mu)\,r^{2}\Bigl(|D_{\hat{t}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{\theta}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{\varphi}}\phi|^{2}+|P(\phi)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(|F_{\hat{t}\hat{\theta}}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{t}\hat{\varphi}}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{r}^{\ast}\hat{\theta}}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{r}^{\ast}\hat{\varphi}}|^{2}\bigr)\Bigr)\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}.

(C.18)

Since
\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t)\leq E^{\hat{t}}(t)\leq\mathcal{E}_{k}(t)\leq\mathcal{E}
and likewise for the commuted energies, we have

E_{0}\ \lesssim\ \mathcal{E}.

(C.19)

Step 4: proof of (
C.15
).

We show the first inequality in (
C.15
); the estimate for
\underline{\alpha}
is identical.
On
\mathbb{S}^{2}
we have the identity (see, e.g.,
[
24
, Appendix]
)

\sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigl|\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}\alpha\bigr|^{2}=|\not{\nabla}\alpha|^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}.

(C.20)

Using the elementary inequality
2(a^{2}+b^{2})\geq(a\pm b)^{2}
and the standard expressions for
\mathrm{div}\,\alpha
and
\mathrm{curl}\,\alpha
in local coordinates, one obtains

|\mathrm{div}\,\alpha|^{2}+|\mathrm{curl}\,\alpha|^{2}\ \leq\ 2\,|\not{\nabla}\alpha|^{2}.

Combining this with (
C.20
) yields the pointwise bound

|\mathrm{div}\,\alpha|^{2}+|\mathrm{curl}\,\alpha|^{2}\ \lesssim\ \sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigl|\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}\alpha\bigr|^{2}.

Integrating in
(r^{\ast},\omega)
with the weight
|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}|
gives (
C.15
).

Step 5: proof of (
C.16
).

By Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
(applied at fixed
(t,r^{\ast})
) we have

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\bigl(|\phi D_{0}\phi|^{2}+|\phi D_{1}\phi|^{2}\bigr)\,d\sigma^{2}\ \lesssim\ \|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\bigl(|D_{0}\phi|^{2}+|D_{1}\phi|^{2}\bigr)\,d\sigma^{2}\ \lesssim\ E_{0},

where we used that
\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}
is controlled by (a finite number of) angular commutations
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}\phi
, and the corresponding
L^{2}
norms are bounded by the reduced energies appearing in
E_{0}
.
Integrating over
r^{\ast}\in[r_{0}^{\ast},R_{0}^{\ast}]
with weight
|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}|
gives (
C.16
).

Combining (
C.3
)–(
C.16
) yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}\ \lesssim\ \int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}E_{0}\,dt\ \lesssim\ t_{i+1}\,E_{0}.

(C.21)

In particular, recalling that the weight
|r^{\ast}-(3M)^{\ast}|
is bounded on the compact region
r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}
, we obtain

\mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}\bigl(t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}<r<R_{0}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ t_{i+1}\Bigl(|\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t_{i})|+|\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t_{i+1})|\Bigr).

(C.22)

Step 6: a localized radial multiplier.

Let
\chi_{\mathrm{mid}}(r^{\ast})
be the smooth cutoff appearing in Proposition
8.2
.
Define a smooth primitive

f(r^{\ast}):=\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\ast}}\chi_{\mathrm{mid}}(s)\,ds,

(C.23)

so that
f
is bounded and
f^{\prime}=\chi_{\mathrm{mid}}
.
Let
\hat{C}:=X_{f}
be the associated radial multiplier from (
8.22
).
Applying the divergence identity (
8.5
) to
J^{\hat{C}}
on the slab
\mathcal{R}_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}
yields

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{|F_{w\theta}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|F_{w\varphi}|^{2}}{r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}+\frac{|F_{v\theta}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|F_{v\varphi}|^{2}}{r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}+|D_{v}\phi|^{2}+|D_{w}\phi|^{2}\Bigr)\chi_{\mathrm{mid}}(r^{\ast})\,r^{2}\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\ \mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}\bigl(t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}<r<R_{0}\bigr)+E^{\hat{C}}(t_{i+1})-E^{\hat{C}}(t_{i}).

(C.24)

(Here we used that
K^{\hat{C}}
is coercive for the terms displayed on the left-hand side, up to terms controlled by
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{C}}
on
r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}
; see
[
24
]
for the standard computation of
T_{\alpha\beta}\pi^{\alpha\beta}(\hat{C})
for radial multipliers.)

Lemma C.2
.

Let
f
be bounded in (
8.22
). Then the associated flux satisfies

|E^{\hat{C}}(t)|\ \lesssim\ E^{\hat{t}}(t).

Proof.

Expanding (
8.23
) for
\hat{C}=X_{f}
gives the coordinate expression

E^{\hat{C}}(t)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-f(r^{\ast})\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}F_{t\theta}F_{r^{\ast}\theta}+\frac{1}{r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}F_{t\varphi}F_{r^{\ast}\varphi}+\Re\bigl(D_{t}\phi\,\overline{D_{r^{\ast}}\phi}\bigr)\right)\,r^{2}\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}.

(C.25)

Using
|f|\leq\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}
together with the inequality
2|ab|\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
and the definition of the
\hat{t}
–energy density, we obtain

|E^{\hat{C}}(t)|\ \lesssim\ \int_{\Sigma_{t}}\Bigl(|F_{\hat{t}A}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{r}^{\ast}A}|^{2}+|D_{\hat{t}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{r}^{\ast}}\phi|^{2}\Bigr)\,d\mu_{\Sigma_{t}}\ \lesssim\ E^{\hat{t}}(t),

as claimed.
∎

Finally, we apply (
C.3
) to the localized fields
(\hat{F},\hat{\phi})
from (
8.24
).
Since the cutoff is supported in the timelike-cone region and
\hat{\chi}_{i}
is bounded, Lemma
C.2
and the definition of
E^{\hat{t}}_{\mathrm{cone}}(t_{i})
yield the desired estimate (
8.28
).
∎

C.4
Proof of Proposition
8.4

Proof of Proposition
8.4
.

Fix
v_{i+1}\geq v_{i}
and set
w_{i}:=t_{i}-r_{1}^{\ast}
.
Split the redshift flux on
\{v=\mathrm{const}\}
into the near-horizon and exterior parts:

\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)=\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\leq r_{1})+\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\geq r_{1}).

Step 1: control of the exterior part.

On
\{r\geq r_{1}\}
the vector fields
\hat{H}
and
\partial_{t}
are uniformly comparable (they differ only by a compactly supported radial term), hence the corresponding flux densities satisfy

J^{\hat{H}}\cdot n_{v}\ \lesssim\ J^{\hat{t}}\cdot n_{v}\qquad\text{on }\{r\geq r_{1}\},

and therefore

\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\geq r_{1})\ \lesssim\ \mathcal{E}^{\hat{t}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\geq r_{1}).

(C.26)

Step 2: control of the near-horizon part by the redshift bulk.

In the redshift region
\{r\leq r_{1}\}
the deformation tensor
\pi(\hat{H})
is positive definite (after the standard cutoff construction of
\hat{H}
in Definition
8.1
).
Consequently, there exists
c=c(M,r_{1})>0
such that

K^{\hat{H}}[F,\phi]\ \geq\ c\,\bigl(J^{\hat{H}}[F,\phi]\cdot n_{v}\bigr)\qquad\text{on }\{r\leq r_{1}\}.

(C.27)

Integrating (
C.27
) over the slab
\{v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1}\}\cap\{w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty\}\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}
and using the coarea formula gives

\int_{v_{i}}^{v_{i+1}}\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\leq r_{1})\,\mathrm{d}v\ \lesssim\ I^{\hat{H}}(v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\leq r_{1}).

(C.28)

Step 3: pigeonhole.

Since
(v_{i+1}-v_{i})\inf_{[v_{i},v_{i+1}]}f\leq\int_{v_{i}}^{v_{i+1}}f
for any nonnegative
f
, we obtain from (
C.28
)

\displaystyle\inf_{v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1}}\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\leq r_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\ \frac{1}{v_{i+1}-v_{i}}\,I^{\hat{H}}(v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)

\displaystyle\bigl(r\leq r_{1}\bigr).

Adding (
C.26
) yields (
8.4
).
∎

C.5
Proof of Proposition
8.5

Proof of Proposition
8.5
.

By definition,
I^{\hat{H}}
is the spacetime integral of the bulk density
K^{\hat{H}}[F,\phi]
, where

K^{\hat{H}}[F,\phi]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,T_{\mu\nu}\,\pi^{\mu\nu}(\hat{H}).

Since
\pi(\hat{H})
is nonnegative on
\{r\leq r_{1}\}
(redshift coercivity), we have

0\leq I^{\hat{H}}(v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\leq r_{1}).

To bound
I^{\hat{H}}
from above, apply the energy identity (
8.19
) on the region

\{v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1}\}\cap\{w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty\}\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}.

All boundary fluxes appearing in (
8.19
) are nonnegative, so dropping the negative contributions gives

\displaystyle I^{\hat{H}}(v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)\bigl(r\leq r_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\ \mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(w=w_{i})(v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v_{i})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty).

Finally, these fluxes are controlled by the global energy bound in Proposition
8.1
(and the bootstrap assumption), noting that
\hat{H}
differs from
\partial_{t}
only by a compactly supported radial term and therefore its flux is uniformly comparable to the
\hat{t}
–flux.
Hence
I^{\hat{H}}(\cdots)\lesssim\mathcal{E}
as claimed.
∎

C.6
Proof of Proposition
8.6

Proof of Proposition
8.6
.

We outline the dyadic redshift argument.

Step 1: exterior flux decay.

The Morawetz/conformal estimates (Section
8.3
) yield decay of the
\hat{t}
–flux through the timelike tube
\{r=r_{1}\}
:

\sup_{v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{i+1}}\mathcal{E}^{\hat{t}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)(r\geq r_{1})\ \lesssim\ \frac{\mathcal{E}}{t_{i}^{2}}.

(C.29)

Step 2: a good ingoing slice.

Apply Proposition
8.4
and use Proposition
8.5
together with (
C.29
) to obtain some
v_{i}^{\sharp}\in[v_{i},v_{i+1}]
such that

\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v_{i}^{\sharp})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)\ \lesssim\ \frac{\mathcal{E}}{v_{i}^{2}}.

(C.30)

(Here we used
v_{i}\simeq t_{i}
along
\{r=r_{1}\}
.)

Step 3: upgrade to arbitrary
v
.

For any
v\in[v_{i},v_{i+1}]
we have
w_{0}(v)=v-2r_{1}^{\ast}\geq w_{i}
, hence

\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{0}(v)\leq w\leq\infty)\leq\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty).

Applying (
8.19
) to the slab
\{v_{i}^{\sharp}\leq\bar{v}\leq v\}\cap\{w\geq w_{i}\}\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}
and using nonnegativity of the bulk term gives

\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)\ \lesssim\ \mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v_{i}^{\sharp})(w_{i}\leq w\leq\infty)+\mathcal{E}^{\hat{t}}(r=r_{1};\ t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}).

The last term is controlled by (
C.29
), so together with (
C.30
) we obtain

\mathcal{E}^{\hat{H}}(v=v)(w_{0}(v)\leq w\leq\infty)\lesssim\mathcal{E}/v_{i}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}/v_{+}^{2}.

This proves (
8.36
).

Step 4: outgoing estimate.

The estimate (
8.37
) follows by applying (
8.19
) to the rectangular region bounded by
\{w=\mathrm{const}\}
and
\{\bar{v}\in[v-1,v]\}
, using (
8.36
) to control the remaining boundary terms.
∎

C.7
Proof of Proposition
8.7

Proof of Proposition
8.7
.

We use the explicit formula (
C.1
) for the bulk density
K^{\hat{K}}
.
The coefficient

2+(3\mu-2)\frac{r^{\ast}}{r}

is positive only in a bounded radial interval: there exist radii
2M<r_{0}<R_{0}<\infty
(depending only on
M
) such that this coefficient is nonnegative for
r\in[r_{0},R_{0}]
.
Restricting to this compact region and using that
t\leq t_{i+1}
on
[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}\bigl(t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim t_{i+1}\!\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\int_{r_{0}^{\ast}}^{R_{0}^{\ast}}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Bigl(|F_{\hat{v}\hat{w}}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}|F_{\hat{\theta}\hat{\varphi}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+|D_{\hat{v}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{w}}\phi|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}|D_{\hat{\theta}}\phi|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}|D_{\hat{\varphi}}\phi|^{2}+P(\phi)\Bigr)\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim t_{i+1}\,\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}\bigl(t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}\bigr).

(C.31)

which is the first inequality in (
8.7
).

Next, by repeating the estimate in the proof of Proposition
8.2
(see (
C.21
)), one obtains the analogue

\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}\bigl(t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ t_{i+1}\,E_{0},

(C.32)

where
E_{0}
is the endpoint reduced-energy quantity defined in (
C.17
).
Inserting (
C.32
) into (
C.7
) gives

\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}\bigl(t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ t_{i+1}^{2}\,E_{0}.

(C.33)

Finally, we relate
E_{0}
to the conformal energies at the endpoints.
Fix
t\in\{t_{i},t_{i+1}\}
and consider the spatial region

|r^{\ast}|\leq\tfrac{3}{4}t_{i}

(where the localized cutoff fields coincide with the original solution on
\Sigma_{t_{i}}
).
On this region we have
v=t+r^{\ast}\geq\tfrac{1}{4}t
and
w=t-r^{\ast}\geq\tfrac{1}{4}t
, hence

\min\{v^{2},w^{2}\}\gtrsim t^{2}.

Using the explicit expression (
C.1
) we therefore obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{3t_{i}}{4}}^{\frac{3t_{i}}{4}}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Bigl(

\displaystyle|F_{\hat{v}\hat{\theta}}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{v}\hat{\varphi}}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{w}\hat{\theta}}|^{2}+|F_{\hat{w}\hat{\varphi}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|D_{\hat{v}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{w}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{\theta}}\phi|^{2}+|D_{\hat{\varphi}}\phi|^{2}+|P(\phi)|\Bigr)\,(1-\mu)\,r^{2}\,dr^{\ast}\,d\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \frac{E^{\hat{K}}(t)}{t^{2}}.

(C.34)

In particular, since the reduced energy
\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t)
in (
C.3
) is dominated by the left-hand side of (
C.7
), we deduce

\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}(t)\ \lesssim\ \frac{E^{\hat{K}}(t)}{t^{2}}.

(C.35)

Commuting with
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}
yields the analogous bound

\tilde{E}^{\hat{t}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t)\ \lesssim\ \frac{E^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t)}{t^{2}}.

(C.36)

Substituting (
C.35
)–(
C.36
) into (
C.33
) gives the second inequality in (
8.7
), completing the proof.
∎

C.8
Proof of Proposition
8.8

Proof of Proposition
8.8
.

Fix
i\geq 0
and let
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
.
Applying the divergence identity (
8.5
) to the multiplier
\hat{K}
on the spacetime region
\mathcal{R}_{[t_{0},t]}
gives

\displaystyle E^{\hat{K}}(t)\leq E^{\hat{K}}(t_{0})+\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}\bigl(t_{0}\leq s\leq t\bigr).

(C.37)

Likewise, commuting with a rotation
\Omega_{j}
and repeating the same argument yields

\displaystyle E^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t)\leq E^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t_{0})+\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}\bigl(t_{0}\leq s\leq t\bigr).

(C.38)

Step 1: bounding
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}
by
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}
.

Decompose
[t_{0},t]
into dyadic subintervals
[t_{m},t_{m+1}]
(with the convention that the last interval may be truncated at
t
).
Using additivity of the bulk term together with (
C.7
) on each dyadic piece and the bound
t_{m+1}\leq t_{i+1}
for
m\leq i
, we obtain

\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}\bigl(t_{0}\leq s\leq t\bigr)\ \lesssim\ t_{i+1}\,\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}\bigl(t_{0}\leq s\leq t\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}\bigr).

(C.39)

(An identical estimate holds for the commuted bulk terms
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}
.)

Step 2: estimating
\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}
.

On each dyadic interval
[t_{m},t_{m+1}]
, the estimate (
C.32
) together with (
C.35
)–(
C.36
) gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}\bigl(t_{m}\leq s\leq t_{m+1}\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ t_{m+1}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\frac{E^{\hat{K}}(t_{m})}{t_{m}^{2}}+\frac{E^{\hat{K}}(t_{m+1})}{t_{m+1}^{2}}

(C.40)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{E^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t_{m})}{t_{m}^{2}}+\frac{E^{\hat{K}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{j}}}(t_{m+1})}{t_{m+1}^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigg).

Summing (
C.40
) for
m=0,\dots,i
and using that
t_{m+1}\leq t_{i+1}
yields the coarse bound

\mathcal{J}^{\hat{G}}\bigl(t_{0}\leq s\leq t_{i+1}\bigr)\bigl(r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{0}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ t_{i+1}\,\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}E^{\hat{t}}\bigl[\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}(F,\phi)\bigr](t_{0}),

(C.41)

where we used that the endpoint conformal energies on the right-hand side of (
C.40
) are finite and controlled by the initial data norm (after commuting with
\mathcal{Z}
up to order
3
).

Step 3: conclusion at dyadic times and extension to all
t
.

Combining (
C.37
), (
C.39
), and (
C.41
) with
t=t_{i+1}
yields the desired control of
E^{\hat{K}}(t_{i+1})
.

For a general time
t\geq t_{0}
, choose
i\geq 0
such that
t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}
(this is possible since
t_{i+1}=(1+\kappa_{0})t_{i}
and
\{t_{i}\}
is an increasing geometric sequence).
Applying (
C.37
) and repeating the preceding argument on
[t_{0},t_{i+1}]
gives the same bound for
E^{\hat{K}}(t)
(up to constants depending only on
\kappa_{0}
).
This proves (
8.41
).
∎
